00:15:05.199 start with the most common which is an element selector and i'm going to select
00:15:10.560 the body element now there is only one body element per page but we have some very small font and i will get into more
00:15:18.959 properties for each of the declarations as we go through tutorials but i'm going to use a few today just for examples and

00:15:37.759 other elements actually inherited from the body element and we'll get more into
00:15:43.120 inheritance in just a little bit too but this in itself is an element selector now
00:15:48.880 there's only one body element per page but say i went ahead and selected the
00:15:53.920 paragraph using the paragraph selector and now i can set the color of the text
00:15:59.839 and i'm going to set the color of the text to purple and i'll save and notice it changed all
00:16:06.320 three of our paragraphs to purple so element selectors select all of the
00:16:11.600 elements of that type so just specifying p for the paragraph element selects
00:16:17.920 all paragraph elements now the second type of selector is a class and so let's define a class selector and
00:16:25.199 i'm going to call this class gray note that a class starts with a period before whatever you name the class and you can

00:16:44.160 now i'll save that and notice paragraphs 2 and 3 turn the gray color and if we

00:16:57.839 class set equal to gray on that paragraph likewise we have a class set equal to
00:17:04.880 gray on the third paragraph as well so classes can be used more than once and
00:17:10.799 they're very useful that way you're creating styles that you can apply throughout your project or throughout
00:17:16.160 your page and then they can be reused with more than one element and note classes are
00:17:21.520 more specific than selecting all paragraphs for example and because
00:17:26.720 they're more specific they overruled the purple setting that we had for all paragraphs and now gray applied to these
00:17:34.400 last two paragraphs here and we will also discuss specificity in more detail
00:17:39.760 in just a few minutes as well now classes are the most common type of selector with css
00:17:45.679 but the more specific selector yet is an id selector and i
00:17:50.880 have an id named second an id attribute inside of the html and here i'm going to
00:17:56.960 apply a font style and we'll set that to italic and i like to have a space afterwards
00:18:04.400 now i'll save and notice the second paragraph became italic as well so it
00:18:10.160 specifically addressed the second paragraph now ids
00:18:15.360 should only exist once in an html document they should be unique in other words however it is not good practice to
00:18:23.360 really use ids inside of your css now it will validate your code has no problem
00:18:30.720 using an id however typically you should use classes and sometimes element selectors but
00:18:38.000 rarely if ever should you use an id selector inside of your css they do have
00:18:43.360 other valid uses if you remember from html linking a form input back to a
00:18:49.200 label element and then as you learn javascript you may have some other uses for id attributes as well but we try to
00:18:56.080 keep them out of css and now that we've covered the three basic types of selectors let's discuss what you can do

00:19:08.160 the color for the h1 to blue and we'll save and we can see it only
00:19:13.280 selected the h1 we only have one h1 on our page but we can also group selectors
00:19:19.200 so let's set all of the h2s as well to blue and you can see i just put a comma
00:19:25.120 between and then this declaration or this rule set the entire rule set if we had more rules in
00:19:31.440 here more declarations i should say it would apply to both the h1 and the h2
00:19:37.679 so you can go ahead and group selectors with a comma in between now notice what
00:19:42.799 happens if i remove the comma and save this declaration or our full rule set
00:19:48.880 especially if we had more declarations none of it would apply to the h1 or the h2 now what's going on
00:19:54.880 here this selector is looking for any h2s that exist inside of an h1 it's not
00:20:02.159 looking for an h1 or an h2 it's only looking for h2s that exist or are nested
00:20:08.799 if you will inside of an h1 so that's why that didn't work that may be useful
00:20:14.080 to know in the future but what we need is a comma in between and then we can select both the h1 and all h2s on the
00:20:22.400 page with this rule set so let's look at an example of where we would select an element that's inside another element so
00:20:29.840 let's start with our paragraph elements again and now we'll look for any span element inside
00:20:36.080 any paragraph element and here let's set a couple of values so we'll set
00:20:41.679 text dash transform and i'm going to choose uppercase here and then i'm also going

00:20:56.400 out we're essentially highlighting what we want to see and now we can see

00:21:06.799 we've set it to uppercase and same with epsom and then we also set gold behind
00:21:13.440 each of these words with this however this doesn't make a lot of sense this is a much better example of where we should

00:21:25.679 highlight and then apply that to our span elements
00:21:31.440 inside of the index so we've come in here and find our span
00:21:38.080 and i'll select both of these and then we can add the class highlight
00:21:48.960 and that makes it reusable if i spell highlight correctly there we go and save and now it's once again applied but this
00:21:56.720 would keep our css much more organized and actually more reusable it's more
00:22:02.640 flexible this way because we might have some span elements doing other things that we didn't want to apply
00:22:09.280 this rule set to and now one last selector to discuss it exists we
00:22:14.400 wouldn't use it that often but it is the universal selector and this means it's
00:22:20.480 selecting everything on the page you typically only see this for what is called a css reset that we will cover in
00:22:27.600 the future but just so you know it exists this selects everything so let's
00:22:32.880 think about what we could apply here that would be applicable to everything
00:22:38.000 so let's change the font and we'll set it to this mono space font
00:22:43.360 and save and now you can see all of the text on the page turned into this monospace font instead

00:22:56.960 that would use that selector but typically that's the only time you see that universal selector used okay let's
00:23:04.080 discuss the cascade css is an acronym for cascading style
00:23:09.360 sheets and essentially that means css works like a waterfall it works from the top down and that means if i were to put
00:23:17.039 in another definition i'll just copy this down for paragraph and where we have
00:23:22.640 the color purple on our first paragraph it really applies to all but then of course the gray class overrides that on
00:23:30.159 the final two but if i came here and said red
00:23:35.360 instead of purple notice how this paragraph turns red and that's because
00:23:41.440 it read this rule set last so first css looked at this at the top it starts at
00:23:47.919 the top and it works its way down and so it read this last essentially this is
00:23:53.039 the last rule and that means whichever definition it reads
00:23:58.159 at the last will be applied now specificity can override this and that's
00:24:03.600 where we talked about elements and then classes classes are more specific for
00:24:08.960 example than just an element selector so we have our class here that has the color gray
00:24:15.520 and it is overriding the red now if we remove this class and we save
00:24:21.279 now they're all red but even though we're looking at the cascade and starting from the top down even if i put
00:24:28.320 this class above the paragraph selectors and save
00:24:33.360 it still applies to these last two paragraphs because it has more specificity
00:24:39.440 than an element selector and the element selector here purple of course is
00:24:44.960 overridden with the last rule being the element selector here for red so we
00:24:50.000 start out with a red paragraph and it's really applied to all paragraphs except
00:24:55.520 that then css sees there is a gray class applied to the second and third paragraphs and
00:25:02.559 that has more specificity so it overrules whatever is set by the element
00:25:08.480 selector now a problem you can have is when you have a large css file and maybe
00:25:13.679 you have set this gray class on these paragraphs and later on let's say you're
00:25:19.360 way down a couple of hundred lines and you're setting a color on a paragraph and you can't understand why it's not
00:25:26.480 being applied so let's go ahead and expand chrome here and you can right
00:25:32.000 click and choose inspect and once you inspect your page
00:25:37.360 then you can look at the different classes and css rules that are applied so here's a good example of that we have
00:25:43.919 got our third paragraph highlighted up here in the elements tab and then we're looking at the styles and
00:25:51.200 we can see we've got paragraph rules here these element selectors but color
00:25:56.400 purple is crossed out and then color red is crossed out so css knows these rule
00:26:02.559 sets exist for paragraph but it also knows it should follow what is the most specific using the rules of specificity
00:26:10.240 so here we have a color gray that is being applied so you can see
00:26:15.360 sometimes when you're struggling with your css and you have written a rule and you're not understanding why it is not
00:26:21.840 being applied you should inspect your code with dev tools and once again you right click and choose inspect to do
00:26:28.960 that and then just go to the correct tabs elements highlight the element that you want look
00:26:34.559 at the styles and you'll see how it is being read by css in the browser or how

00:27:12.400 is parent to every other element here and so when we set the font size all of these other
00:27:18.640 elements inherited that font size now typically anything related to font or
00:27:24.240 typography is inherited and that can include things like color line height alignment all
00:27:31.039 sorts of settings dealing with the font and the typography however any properties that are not related to those

00:56:02.400 parent for the h1 it is inside of a header element so if we take this header element
00:56:08.640 and we set it to a width of 50 percent then we should see a difference and yes
00:56:15.040 it's even smaller so we're taking up only fifty percent of the header element
00:56:20.079 which is taking up fifty percent of the page so really this h1 would be twenty
00:56:25.119 five percent of the page likewise if we change this to a hundred for the header
00:56:30.240 then we'll see our h1 takes up fifty percent of the page so that's the thing to remember about setting a percentage
00:56:37.680 for the width is that you are putting it relative to the parent it's only using
00:56:43.200 what it has available to it if the header is a hundred percent then the h1 that is the child has a
00:56:49.599 hundred percent of the page available to it however if the header is smaller let's put it at eighty percent
00:56:56.640 then we're only using up 50 of 80 of the page and once again if you
00:57:02.960 want an element that is a block level element to take up 100 of the width of the page you don't
00:57:08.319 really need to set it because that is the default value already so now we're taking up half of the page by setting
00:57:15.119 the h1 to 50 because it is 50 of the full 100 width and you may see
00:57:22.480 other uses for percentages as well but you commonly see them to set the sizing
00:57:28.400 of different elements as far as the width of the page or sometimes the height but also we don't need to set the
00:57:35.359 height because it will grow as the content grows i'm going to go back to my settings and set it from very
00:57:42.400 large back to medium which is where most have their browser default to now when
00:57:47.599 we look at our page the font is smaller but let's talk about another relative
00:57:52.880 unit and this one does show on the mdn page in the chart down here under relative length units and it's the rim
00:58:00.319 this is the unit that we'll typically use for font size and it relates to the font size of the root element now i
00:58:07.359 already suggested we shouldn't set a font size on the root element so for our
00:58:13.200 html element we typically wouldn't set a font size in here because we want to let the browser
00:58:20.079 handle that and set the default font size so let's not do this for the html
00:58:25.599 element i'll just go ahead and cut this i'm going to put it below the h1 and i'm going to set a font size for our
00:58:32.799 paragraphs instead and this font size if we set it to one ram
00:58:37.839 that's already the default so it shouldn't change anything and we see the size is the same so it says one root
00:58:45.040 element which the root element is the default font size set by the browser the em doesn't stand for element by the way
00:58:51.839 that's m so we have one root em or one root m
00:58:57.040 but that means the root size that is defined and in our case when we don't
00:59:02.240 set it then it's set by the browser so hopefully that clears up some confusion but if we put in two m's then it's going
00:59:08.880 to double the default size and now we're probably at 36 pixels right here because
00:59:14.400 most browsers default to that 16 pixel size so you can see how this relates
00:59:20.000 back to the root now if we set a font size on the parent because our paragraph
00:59:25.040 is in a main element we set this font size to 2 rim also it doesn't change the
00:59:33.680 paragraph font size at all because the font set here is not relative to the main
00:59:39.839 element it's not relative to the parent it's relative to the root which is defined by the browser so it's always
00:59:46.480 going to look back to that default root size if we had set a font size in the
00:59:51.760 html element it would be looking at that but without setting it it's the default size
00:59:57.520 so what happens though if we switch these units if we're not using rem
01:00:02.880 if we're using m just em so now i'll delete the r there and save oh boy
01:00:11.200 that got a lot bigger well here's the problem that we would have if we were using ms for our font size it gets
01:00:18.079 amplified so our main element was looking at the root so it doubled that
01:00:24.000 to 32 pixels and then using two m's is looking at the main element which is
01:00:30.720 the parent of the paragraph and so then it doubled that so now we're at 64 pixels so that's what happens and you
01:00:38.319 can get unintended consequences if you just use m's and that's why using the rim and always looking at the root
01:00:45.440 definitely helps because the way we can get confused for example is if i remove
01:00:50.559 this setting on the main and i save well now this is also looking at the root because there's no other font size saved
01:00:57.359 and so we think okay we're good to use just m's but the problem is if somewhere along the way in our
01:01:04.000 project and we have many elements we're styling we set another font size on a parent
01:01:09.520 element suddenly it's amplified when we use m's so i'm going to switch this back to two rims and get closer to the size
01:01:16.960 we'd want and it's going to look at the root so when would we use m's instead of rims we know we want to
01:01:23.599 use rems on font size well when we set the font size on an element

01:01:35.599 which i know we haven't covered and we will in more detail when we look at the css box model but i'm going to go ahead

02:08:25.760 classes so this has more specificity and that's why the visited link continues to
02:08:32.079 stay purple even though the anchor element comes afterwards in the cascade
02:08:37.199 but that's not usually the order we want things we usually kind of want to think about it in the order that we would have

02:08:48.320 have the same specificity and one can overwrite the other so we really have to

02:09:43.840 then your pseudo class of visited then your pseudo class of hover and finally your pseudo class of active

02:50:47.279 and we've talked about this being already as a default in line
02:50:52.800 and then of course paragraphs default to block and many other elements as well
02:50:58.160 like the main element we talked about however there is another option and that is inline dash block and let's see what
02:51:05.520 happens when we apply that to our opposite class now our padding is still applied but it's not overlapping the
02:51:12.080 other paragraph so it is now respecting the top and the bottom likewise if we come back
02:51:18.399 and apply that margin and let's just say margin dash top it could be bottom as well but we'll just put 100 pixels for
02:51:25.600 the top and that worked as well now you can see we have extra space here and again this
02:51:30.800 is applying just to that span element so not to the full paragraph itself and now we could even add the height that we
02:51:37.200 applied earlier that didn't work and we say 200 pixels and now our span element just got a lot taller
02:51:44.800 so the inline block property value is kind of a hybrid here because we stayed in line in our
02:51:51.279 paragraph this didn't create a new line like block would and at the same time it
02:51:56.880 now allows some properties to be applied that normally wouldn't apply to the
02:52:02.560 inline display so to quickly recap block level elements stack on top of each
02:52:08.240 other and always create a new line inline elements do not stack on top of
02:52:13.359 each other and do not create a new line block level elements automatically have a 100 width of whatever they are given
02:52:21.600 if they're not inside of something that is limiting their width they will be the full width of the page inline level
02:52:28.160 elements only take up the width of their content of course unless we put extra padding on them and then it has a little
02:52:34.319 bit more width here because of that padding and then when we switch to inline block we get kind of a hybrid

05:32:14.160 let's move on to css media queries media queries allow you to modify your site based on specific characteristics
05:32:21.360 and parameters and most often we look at the browser viewport width this is key to responsive design as our
05:32:27.600 web pages will respond to the width of a device viewport so let's look at the starter code i have got some basic html
05:32:34.958 here and i have a page outline that has a header a nav inside the header element
05:32:41.200 a main element and a footer element and so we're just going to outline a page and see how it changes based on viewport
05:32:49.680 changes and then in the css i've got some basic css here we're importing the nenito font from google other than that
05:32:58.000 we have the basic css reset and a basic style a change you may notice is i'm
05:33:03.680 using the font shorthand property now that sets both the font size and the font family all in one right
05:33:10.480 here in the body other than that min height of 100 viewport units okay before we do anything with css we really need
05:33:18.160 to look at the html there is a meta tag here that enables responsive design and
05:33:24.400 that meta tag has the name viewport you can see the content is width equals device width and then
05:33:31.920 initial scale of 1.0 this is necessary to enable our media queries and allow
05:33:37.840 responsive design in our web pages now do you have to memorize how to type that out not really because vs code helps us
05:33:45.280 a lot i'm going to just select everything quickly with control a cut it out of the page with control x so

05:34:40.000 have let's just talk about the syntax of a media query first so i'm going to paste this in and look at the syntax
05:34:46.400 that is how we write it so we start out with app media and then we give the media type and then
05:34:52.638 we specify a condition or conditions as we can link those with logical operators
05:34:58.000 and and or and then we specify a breakpoint and that depends on the condition as what
05:35:04.558 type of break point we'll use there's lots of different types of break points and conditions we can apply and then
05:35:11.040 just like we do with anything else we put the css rules between the curly braces so now that we've looked at that
05:35:17.440 syntax let's go ahead and write a media query and see how that applies so we can
05:35:22.878 start out with at media and instead of media type here we can say
05:35:28.480 screen that is the most common one you'll see you might also see all or print or even speech for screen readers
05:35:36.320 and devices that read the screen back to you and then after condition or as the
05:35:41.440 condition i should say you often see min width and you should read min width as
05:35:47.600 starting from so whatever the width we specify is these styles will start from
05:35:54.320 that width when we say min width likewise max width would be up 2 so the
05:36:00.080 styles would apply up to the width we would provide if we said max width there
05:36:05.360 we usually do this with min width and that is because we design from the smallest to the largest and that is what
05:36:12.400 is called mobile first design and responsive design and the reason we do that is well think
05:36:18.638 about it this way as an analogy when you get a box you unpack something it's
05:36:23.760 often hard to put everything back in the box the way it was packed and get the lid to close it's kind of like that when
05:36:29.760 we design a page it's easier to start with the smallest and work our way towards the largest rather than start
05:36:36.080 with the largest and then try to squeeze everything into a smaller and smaller box if you will so that's kind of the
05:36:42.160 analogy i use to explain that so we'll start out with a mobile device screen usually that's a one column design and
05:36:49.200 we can spread things out as we go so for this first min width for the break point
05:36:54.638 let's say i would put and remember this is starting from so i would put something like oh let's
05:37:01.680 go with 481 pixels so all of our styles before this
05:37:07.840 media query would be applied until we got to 481
05:37:12.958 pixels and then they would still be applied unless we were to overwrite them because
05:37:18.718 the cascade still works so here we could change something maybe about the body maybe the font maybe a background color
05:37:26.480 and that's what we'll experiment with today because it's so easy to see those background color changes
05:37:31.600 and then we can overwrite the previous style we had other styles if we do not
05:37:36.878 overwrite those they will still apply if they were applied before the media query now as always i'll give a link to the
05:37:43.760 mdn references in the description below but let's talk about some of the other conditions and break points we could
05:37:50.080 have although as i've said min width and max width with pixels as a breakpoint is
05:37:55.200 very common but you could also have something like orientation
05:38:00.320 and then you would set that to possibly landscape which would be kind
05:38:05.520 of turning your phone to the horizontal instead of the vertical orientation something like that there's also
05:38:12.480 something like min dash aspect dash ratio and there you can actually
05:38:20.400 set a ratio and there are many different sizes of phones so while landscape would
05:38:26.160 actually apply to anything that was wider than it was tall here we could set
05:38:31.440 something more like specific like 16 by 9 or say
05:38:36.798 7 by 4 something like that then when the aspect ratio reached that size so the

05:39:59.680 common media query breakpoints you can see mobile devices are often considered to be
05:40:05.400 481 pixels width or less and then from 481 to 768 usually ipads
05:40:13.600 tablets 769 to 1024 small screens and laptops
05:40:19.520 1025 to 1200 desktops and large screens and 1201 and greater extra large screens
05:40:27.440 and tvs now that's just kind of a common general guideline of course there are no
05:40:32.558 standards so you can set these how you want but let's look at what the bootstrap css framework does

05:40:44.760 576 instead of 481 as we saw referenced above is considered extra small and then
05:40:51.680 anything greater than 576 is small 768 and above is medium
05:40:58.920 992 is large 1200 is extra large and then they have a second extra-large
05:41:04.480 which is double xl at 1400. now tailwind is another css framework that is very
05:41:11.840 popular now in recent times and you can see they've also differed somewhat they
05:41:17.120 consider the extra small to be less than 640. maybe that's because in recent years our
